## This Session's Flow

### Starting State
The session resumed from a prior conversation where we were about to implement the fix for timeline drag instant snap-back in `usePositionManagement.ts`. The root cause was identified: `applyOptimistic` was inside `runSerializedTimelineWrite`, so when the queue was blocked by an 8-12s batch editor RPC, timeline drags had no visual update.

### Fix 1: Timeline drag snap-back (applyOptimistic before queue)
The user's primary issue: timeline drags snap back instantly, batch editor drags kind of stay but revert on refresh.

**Root cause**: In `usePositionManagement.ts`, `setFramePositions` wrapped ALL of `positionSystem.updatePositions()` inside `runSerializedTimelineWrite`. Since `applyOptimistic` was inside `updatePositions`, the visual update was gated behind the write queue. When the queue was blocked by an 8-12s midpoint RPC, no visual update fired.

**Fix implemented**:
1. In `useTimelinePositions.ts`:
   - Added `externalRollback?: (() => void) | null` to `UpdateOptions`
   - Added `applyOptimisticPositionUpdate` method (exposed in return)
   - Modified change calculation in `updatePositions` to use `snapshotRef.current` when `skipOptimistic: true` (because after pre-optimistic apply, `positionsRef.current` reflects new positions, making diff = 0 and skipping DB write)
   - Threaded `externalRollback` into rollback variable

2. In `usePositionManagement.ts`:
   - Modified `setFramePositions` to call `positionSystem.applyOptimisticPositionUpdate(newPositions)` BEFORE `runSerializedTimelineWrite`
   - Passes `skipOptimistic: true, externalRollback: preAppliedRollback` to `updatePositions`

### User challenge: "WHY IS THAT RPC SO SLOW? THIS WAS INSTANTANEOUS BEFORE"
User interrupted while we were debugging, asking why the RPC is so slow.

**Investigation findings**:
- DB executes `batch_update_timeline_frames` in 5.6-14.9ms (from psql timing)
- API responds in 119ms from curl
- 70 total DB connections, max 120, 60 idle, no lock waiters
- Triggers on shot_generations: `sync_shot_generations_jsonb_row` (AFTER INSERT/DELETE ROW), `sync_shot_generations_update_batch` (AFTER UPDATE STATEMENT with SKIP LOCKED)
- Triggers on generations cascade: `audit_shot_data_trigger` (AFTER INSERT/UPDATE ROW), `trg_sync_variant_from_generation` (AFTER UPDATE ROW - no-op for shot_data changes)
- Confirmed SKIP LOCKED migration deployed on live DB

**Key finding from log analysis**: The RPC fetch never returns. Not a DB timeout (which would give a 5xx error), not invalid data (which would fail fast). The fetch hangs indefinitely from the browser but works from curl in 119ms.

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **Timeline drag path** (`usePositionManagement` → `useTimelinePositions`): applies optimistic updates, then writes to DB via `persistTimelineFrameBatch`
   - **Batch editor path** (`useEnhancedShotImageReorder` → `moveItemsToMidpoint`): DB-first write, no optimistic update
   - **`runSerializedTimelineWrite`**: module-level per-shot write queue; both paths share it; `shotWriteDepth` tracks depth
   - **`applyOptimistic`**: immediate visual state update inside `useTimelinePositions`; was gated behind write queue — root cause of timeline snap-back
   - **`snapshotRef.current`**: pre-optimistic positions snapshot set by `applyOptimistic`; needed to compute correct diff when `skipOptimistic: true`
   - **`applyOptimisticPositionUpdate`**: new method exposed from `useTimelinePositions` that applies visual update before entering the queue
   - **`pendingUpdatesRef`**: protects optimistic positions across async syncs; prevents `syncFromDatabase` from overwriting optimistic state
   - **`batch_update_timeline_frames`**: SECURITY DEFINER set-based PostgreSQL RPC; authenticated role has `statement_timeout=8s`
   - **`sync_shot_data_update_batch`**: AFTER UPDATE STATEMENT trigger on `shot_generations` with `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` (confirmed deployed)
   - **RPC hang**: the `supabase.rpc()` fetch never returns a response from the browser even though curl completes in 119ms and DB executes in 5-15ms; root cause unknown

     ```typescript
     const basePositions =
       skipOptimistic && snapshotRef.current ? snapshotRef.current : positionsRef.current;
     const changes: Array<{ id: string; oldPos: number | null; newPos: number }> = [];
     for (const [id, newPos] of quantizedPositions) {
       const oldPos = basePositions.get(id);
       if (oldPos !== newPos) {
         changes.push({ id, oldPos: oldPos ?? null, newPos });
       }
     }
     ```
     - Threaded `externalRollback` into rollback variable:
     ```typescript
     let rollback: (() => void) | null = null;
     if (!skipOptimistic) {

       rollback = externalRollback;
     }
     ```
     - Added `applyOptimisticPositionUpdate` method (drag-path: no quantization, computes changes from `positionsRef.current`, calls `applyOptimistic`):
     ```typescript
     const applyOptimisticPositionUpdate = useCallback((
       newPositions: Map<string, number>,
     ): (() => void) | null => {
       const updates: Array<{ id: string; position: number; operation: 'add' | 'move' | 'remove' }> = [];
       for (const [id, newPos] of newPositions) {
         const oldPos = positionsRef.current.get(id);
         if (oldPos !== newPos) {
           updates.push({
             id,
             position: newPos,
             operation: oldPos === undefined ? 'add' : 'move',
           });
         }
       }
       if (updates.length === 0) return null;
       return applyOptimistic(updates);
     }, [applyOptimistic]);
     ```
     - Added `applyOptimisticPositionUpdate` to return object

**1. Run it directly in the browser console** — bypasses all app code:

If this also hangs, the problem is the supabase client or the network, not app code. If it works, the problem is in how the app is calling it.

**2. Check the Chrome Network tab** while a drag hang is in progress — look for the `batch_update_timeline_frames` request. The key question is whether it shows:
- **Stalled** → browser can't open a connection (connection pool exhausted — 6 HTTP/1.1 connections to supabase.co already open)
- **Waiting (TTFB)** → request was sent but PostgREST is not responding

**3. Add logging to `fetchWithTimeout`** so we can see if the fetch even gets called for RPC requests:

```typescript
if (!isEdgeFunction && !isStorageUpload) {
  console.log('[fetchWithTimeout] RPC passthrough', url, !!init?.signal, init?.signal?.aborted);
  return fetch(input, init);
}
```

My current best guess is **connection pool exhaustion** — the realtime WebSocket, background queries (React Query polling), and prior hung requests can exhaust the browser's 6 HTTP/1.1 connections to `supabase.co`. Each hung RPC occupies a slot permanently, and after the first hang, all subsequent drags queue behind it at the browser level, never starting. The Network tab "Stalled" status would confirm this in seconds.

Now every REST/RPC fetch logs three events:

- `start` — with a short `fetchId`, whether the signal is active/already-aborted/none, and the URL slug
- `settled` — with HTTP status and duration
- `error` — with error name and duration

For a hung drag you'll see the `start` line for `batch_update_timeline_frames` but no `settled`/`error` following it. For the "Stalled" connection-pool hypothesis, you'd see the `start` line never appear at all (or appear much later than the optimistic log), because the fetch isn't even handed to the browser's network stack yet.

The `fetchId` ties each start→settled/error pair so you can track concurrent requests independently.

### Added fetchWithTimeout logging

### New log localhost-1771459829911

### New log localhost-1771460047426
Critical finding: `[fetchWithTimeout] start batch_update_timeline_frames` NEVER appears even though watchdog fires at 8002ms. The hang is INSIDE the supabase client, before `fetch` is even called.

### Root cause found: navigator.locks in GoTrue
Traced the call chain:
1. `SupabaseClient.js` line 73: `this.fetch = fetchWithAuth(supabaseKey, this._getAccessToken.bind(this), settings.global.fetch)`
2. `fetchWithAuth` calls `_getAccessToken()` → `this.auth.getSession()` BEFORE calling `fetchWithTimeout`
3. `GoTrueClient._acquireLock()` uses `navigator.locks` (exclusive) in browser environments
4. If the lock is held by a concurrent operation, `getSession()` queues indefinitely (`acquireTimeout = -1`)
5. The `pendingInLock` queue + while loop can hold the lock for an extended time
6. During this time, ALL `supabase.rpc()` calls hang before reaching `fetchWithTimeout`

### Storage key discovered
`SupabaseClient.js` line 52: `const defaultStorageKey = 'sb-${new URL(this.authUrl).hostname.split('.')[0]}-auth-token'`

The fix being implemented: provide a custom `accessToken` function via `createClient({ accessToken: ... })` that bypasses `getSession()` and the navigator.locks entirely. Cache the token via `onAuthStateChange`.

We were in the middle of investigating the storage key format when the summary was requested.

## Key files modified:
1. `src/shared/lib/timelineFrameBatchPersist.ts` - added standalone payload logging, UUID/int validation
2. `src/tools/travel-between-images/components/Timeline/hooks/usePositionManagement.ts` - pre-queue optimistic update
3. `src/tools/travel-between-images/components/Timeline/hooks/useTimelinePositions.ts` - added applyOptimisticPositionUpdate, snapshotRef fix for skipOptimistic
4. `src/integrations/supabase/bootstrap/fetchWithTimeout.ts` - added RPC logging (currently filtering to /rpc/ only)

## The fix to implement:
In `createSupabaseClient.ts`, provide `accessToken` option to bypass navigator.locks. Need to:
1. Cache current token
2. Update via `onAuthStateChange`
3. Initialize from localStorage directly using key `sb-${projectRef}-auth-token`

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:
   - Fix timeline drag instant snap-back (positions snap back immediately when user drags timeline nodes)
   - Investigate why `batch_update_timeline_frames` RPC never completes from the browser (fetch hangs indefinitely)
   - Validate data being sent to the RPC ("what data are we calling it with? How do we validate that we're even using the right data type?")
   - User stated: "I think you're sending the wrong kind of id" — traced whether IDs were `shot_generations.id` vs `generations.id`
   - User: "why not try run it with this precise data?" — run exact payload from log against DB
   - User: "so what could be unique about running it in the browser? how can you get to the bottom of it?" — add browser-side fetch logging

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **Timeline drag snap-back fix**: `applyOptimisticPositionUpdate` must be called BEFORE `runSerializedTimelineWrite` so visual update happens immediately regardless of queue state
   - **`navigator.locks` (Web Locks API)**: GoTrue v2 uses exclusive `navigator.locks` to serialize auth operations. `getSession()` acquires this lock. When held by a background operation (e.g. token refresh or concurrent React Query fetches draining `pendingInLock`), ALL `supabase.rpc()` calls queue indefinitely before `fetch` is even called
   - **`fetchWithAuth` wraps `fetchWithTimeout`**: `SupabaseClient.fetch = fetchWithAuth(supabaseKey, _getAccessToken, fetchWithTimeout)`. Every request calls `_getAccessToken()` → `getSession()` → `_acquireLock(-1, ...)` BEFORE calling `fetchWithTimeout`. Lock timeout = -1 means wait forever.
   - **`pendingInLock` queue**: Inside the navigator.locks callback, concurrent `_acquireLock` calls are serialized via a `pendingInLock` array + while-loop drain. If many React Query queries arrive concurrently, the while loop holds the navigator.locks lock for the entire drain duration. New arrivals extend the queue continuously.
   - **`skipOptimistic: true` + `snapshotRef`**: After pre-applying optimistic update, `positionsRef.current` already reflects new positions. `updatePositions` must use `snapshotRef.current` (pre-optimistic snapshot) to compute DB diff, or it sees zero changes and skips the write.
   - **`runSerializedTimelineWrite`**: Module-level per-shot write queue. Both timeline drag and batch editor paths share it. `shotWriteDepth` tracks depth.
   - **`batch_update_timeline_frames` RPC**: SECURITY DEFINER set-based CTE that authenticates via `auth.uid()` → joins `shot_generations → shots → projects`. Executes in 47-60ms from psql. Completely fine at DB level.
   - **Storage key format**: `sb-${new URL(authUrl).hostname.split('.')[0]}-auth-token` (e.g., `sb-wczysqzxlwdndgxitrvc-auth-token`)

     ```typescript
     const applyOptimisticPositionUpdate = useCallback((
       newPositions: Map<string, number>,
     ): (() => void) | null => {
       const updates = [];
       for (const [id, newPos] of newPositions) {
         const oldPos = positionsRef.current.get(id);
         if (oldPos !== newPos) {
           updates.push({ id, position: newPos, operation: oldPos === undefined ? 'add' : 'move' });

       }

     }, [applyOptimistic]);
     ```

     ```typescript
     export function fetchWithTimeout(input: URL | RequestInfo, init: RequestInit = {}): Promise<Response> {
       const url = getRequestUrl(input);
       const isEdgeFunction = url.includes('/functions/v1/');
       const isStorageUpload = url.includes('/storage/v1/object/');

       if (!isEdgeFunction && !isStorageUpload) {
         const isRpc = url.includes('/rpc/');
         if (isRpc) {
           const callerSignalState = init.signal instanceof AbortSignal
             ? (init.signal.aborted ? 'already-aborted' : 'active')
             : 'none';
           const urlSlug = url.split('?')[0].split('/').pop() ?? url;
           const fetchId = Math.random().toString(36).slice(2, 7);
           console.log(`[fetchWithTimeout] start ${urlSlug} id=${fetchId} signal=${callerSignalState}`);
           const startedAt = Date.now();
           return fetch(input, init).then(
             (res) => {
               console.log(`[fetchWithTimeout] settled ${urlSlug} id=${fetchId} status=${res.status} durationMs=${Date.now() - startedAt}`);
               return res;
             },
             (err: unknown) => {
               const name = err instanceof Error ? err.name : String(err);
               console.log(`[fetchWithTimeout] error ${urlSlug} id=${fetchId} name=${name} durationMs=${Date.now() - startedAt}`);
               throw err;
             },
           );
         }
         return fetch(input, init);
       }
       // ... edge function / storage path unchanged
     }
     ```

     - Key code (lines 729-783):
     ```javascript
     async _acquireLock(acquireTimeout, fn) {
       if (this.lockAcquired) {
         // re-entrant: add to pendingInLock queue, serialize after last item
         const last = this.pendingInLock.length
           ? this.pendingInLock[this.pendingInLock.length - 1]
           : Promise.resolve();
         const result = (async () => { await last; return await fn(); })();
         this.pendingInLock.push(...);
         return result;
       }
       return await this.lock(`lock:${this.storageKey}`, acquireTimeout, async () => {
         this.lockAcquired = true;
         const result = fn();
         this.pendingInLock.push(...);
         await result;
         while (this.pendingInLock.length) {
           const waitOn = [...this.pendingInLock];
           await Promise.all(waitOn);
           this.pendingInLock.splice(0, waitOn.length);
         }
         return await result;
       });
     }
     ```

Diffing against it gives zero changes → early return → no DB write. Fixed by diffing against `snapshotRef.current` (set synchronously by `applyOptimistic` before state update) when `skipOptimistic: true`.

5. Problem Solving:
   - **Timeline snap-back**: FIXED. Pre-apply optimistic update before write queue. Confirmed working in logs: `optimistic pre-applied {hasChanges: true}` and `positions state changed {caller: 'applyOptimistic'}`.
   - **ID type investigation**: Confirmed correct. `shotGen.id` = `shot_generations.id` per `GenerationRow` type. IDs exist in `shot_generations` table. RPC works from psql in 60ms.
   - **RPC hang root cause**: IDENTIFIED. Not network, not DB, not data. The hang is in `GoTrueClient._acquireLock()` using `navigator.locks`. Before `fetchWithTimeout` is called, `_getAccessToken()` → `getSession()` acquires an exclusive navigator.locks lock. When this lock is held by background React Query queries draining `pendingInLock`, the drag's `getSession()` call queues indefinitely.
   - **Evidence**: In `localhost-1771460047426.log`, `[fetchWithTimeout] start batch_update_timeline_frames` never appears even though watchdog fires at 8002ms — confirming the hang is pre-fetch, inside `_getAccessToken()`.
   - **Direct console test worked (72ms)**: When called directly (`window.supabase.rpc(...)` with no concurrent load), the lock isn't contended and the request completes immediately.

   Root cause fully confirmed:
   - `this.fetch = fetchWithAuth(supabaseKey, _getAccessToken, fetchWithTimeout)` — every request calls `_getAccessToken()` first
   - `_getAccessToken()` → `this.auth.getSession()` → `_acquireLock(-1, fn)` — waits indefinitely for navigator.locks
   - `navigator.locks` is exclusive and held during the entire `pendingInLock` drain loop
   - When many React Query queries run concurrently, the drain loop holds the lock for the full duration
   - Drag's `getSession()` queues behind this lock → `fetchWithTimeout` never reached

   The fix: maintain a `cachedToken` variable, update it via `onAuthStateChange`, and provide `accessToken: async () => cachedToken` in `createClient()`.

The anon key is still there — `SUPABASE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY` is imported from env and used in two places in the new code:

1. **Line 64**: `headers.set('apikey', SUPABASE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY)` — the `apikey` header (required by PostgREST)
2. **Line 66**: `Bearer ${cachedToken ?? SUPABASE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY}` — falls back to anon key if no user session (same fallback `fetchWithAuth` uses)

The original `fetchWithAuth` in supabase-js does exactly this: sets `apikey` to the anon key and sets `Authorization` to `Bearer ${accessToken ?? anonKey}`. We're replicating the same logic, just without the `getSession()` call.

Here's the summary of the fix:

---

**Root cause**: Every `supabase.rpc()` / `supabase.from()` call goes through `fetchWithAuth → _getAccessToken() → GoTrueClient.getSession() → _acquireLock(-1) → navigator.locks` (exclusive, cross-tab). When a concurrent token refresh holds this lock (and our `fetchWithTimeout` has no timeout for auth URLs), the drag's RPC queues behind it indefinitely — explaining why the watchdog fired at 8s but `[fetchWithTimeout] start` never appeared.

**Fix**: `PostgrestClient.fetch` is a public property that's read dynamically on each `from()`/`rpc()` call. After client construction, we replace it with `dataFetch` — a function that reads the `cachedToken` variable directly and injects it as the `Authorization` header, completely bypassing `getSession()`/navigator.locks. The cache is seeded from localStorage at startup (before `onAuthStateChange` fires) and kept fresh via `onAuthStateChange`.

**What's unchanged**: `supabase.auth.*` operations, realtime auth (uses `_getAccessToken` for WebSocket headers), functions/storage (still use the original `this.fetch` via getters), and all auth-related network requests.

> DEVELOPER

how confident ar eyou that this was the problem?

> AGENT

Honest answer: **high confidence on the symptom, moderate confidence on the exact trigger**.

**Very confident (near certain)**:
- The hang is definitively pre-`fetchWithTimeout`. The watchdog fires at 8s but `[fetchWithTimeout] start` never appears. That eliminates: network issues, DB latency, the RPC itself, timeouts inside our code. The only place between "RPC called" and "fetchWithTimeout reached" is `fetchWithAuth → _getAccessToken() → getSession() → _acquireLock()`. There's no other code path there.

**Fairly confident**:
- The lock mechanism is navigator.locks (that's what GoTrueClient uses in browsers)
- It's the `_acquireLock(-1)` path (wait-forever) that causes the indefinite hang
- The fix addresses the right layer

**Less certain**:
- What was actually holding the lock at that specific moment. The two most plausible theories: (1) a concurrent token refresh triggered by `__loadSession` finding the token within EXPIRY_MARGIN_MS, or (2) many concurrent React Query queries pile into `pendingInLock` and the drain takes longer than expected. I can't tell from the logs which one.
- Why it didn't affect the `update_tool_settings_atomic` calls that ran just before. Those also go through `_acquireLock`. If the lock were held continuously, those would have hung too. Something changed between line 28 and line 60 in the log that started holding the lock — I'm inferring "ShotEditor mount triggers a burst of queries that holds the lock" but can't confirm.

**The fix is sound regardless**: bypassing `getSession()` for data requests eliminates the entire class of problem. Even if my exact theory about the trigger is wrong, the fix correctly removes the dependency on the auth lock for data reads.

The segment slots computed from this call use LIVE DB positions for index computation, not local/optimistic positions.

The structural issue: `buildDisplaySlots` in `useSegmentOutputStrip` matches segments to pairs using `pairDataByIndex.get(pair.index).startImage.id` → `byPsgId.get(startImageId)`. When images are REORDERED (dragged past each other), the `startImage.id` for pair 0 changes to a different image, and the segment (whose `pairShotGenerationId` still points to the original start image) can't be matched → segment disappears.

The data flow split:
- Pixel positions: from `pairInfo` (LOCAL/optimistic) ✓
- Segment slot matching: from `pairDataByIndex` (LOCAL) + `rawSegmentSlots` (from `useSegmentOutputsForShot` with LIVE positions)
- When reordering: LOCAL pair 0 start image changes, but segment's `pairShotGenerationId` was recorded with original start → mismatch

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **navigator.locks bypass (FIXED)**: `PostgrestClient.fetch` is a public property read dynamically on each `from()`/`rpc()` call. We replaced it post-construction with a `dataFetch` function that reads a cached token directly, bypassing `GoTrueClient.getSession() → _acquireLock(-1) → navigator.locks`.
   - **Token cache**: Seeded from localStorage at startup using key `sb-${projectRef}-auth-token`, kept fresh via `client.auth.onAuthStateChange`.
   - **Segment position data flow**: Segment pixel positions come from `pairInfo` (derived from optimistic `currentPositions`). Segment SLOT MATCHING comes from `buildDisplaySlots` which uses `pairDataByIndex.get(pair.index).startImage.id` to look up segments by `pairShotGenerationId`.
   - **Two-data-source split**: 
     - `pairInfo` + `pairDataByIndex` = computed from LOCAL/optimistic positions in `useComputedTimelineData`
     - `segmentSlots` (rawSegmentSlots) = computed from `useSegmentOutputsForShot` called with `undefined` for `localShotGenPositions` in `useShotEditorController.ts`
   - **Reorder vs. move-within-pair**: Moving an image within a pair (not crossing another) → pair indices unchanged → segment lookup still works. Reordering (image crosses another) → pair 0's `startImage.id` changes → segment can't be found (its `pairShotGenerationId` still references the old start image) → segment disappears.
   - **`dynamicPositions`**: `calculateDragPreview()` in `useTimelineDrag` — returns `framePositions` when `currentMousePosRef.current = null` (not dragging), returns `frozenPositions` during the 500ms drop sync window when `dropState.isDropping = true`.
   - **`displayPositions`**: `positionSystem.positions` from `useTimelinePositions` (optimistic state updated by `applyOptimisticPositionUpdate`). Passed as `framePositions` prop to `TimelineContainer`.

     - Replaces `PostgrestClient.fetch` post-construction to bypass `getSession()/navigator.locks` for all data requests
     - Cached token seeded from localStorage, kept fresh via `onAuthStateChange`
     ```typescript
     function readTokenFromStorage(): string | null {
       if (typeof window === 'undefined') return null;
       try {
         const projectRef = new URL(SUPABASE_URL).hostname.split('.')[0];
         const raw = localStorage.getItem(`sb-${projectRef}-auth-token`);
         if (!raw) return null;
         const parsed = JSON.parse(raw) as { access_token?: string };
         return parsed?.access_token ?? null;
       } catch {
         return null;
       }
     }
     
     export function createSupabaseClient() {
       try {
         let cachedToken: string | null = readTokenFromStorage();
         const client = createClient<Database>(SUPABASE_URL, SUPABASE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY, {
           auth: { autoRefreshToken: true, persistSession: true, detectSessionInUrl: true },
           realtime: {
             params: { eventsPerSecond: 10 },
             heartbeatIntervalMs: REALTIME_HEARTBEAT_INTERVAL_MS,
             reconnectAfterMs: (tries: number) => Math.min(tries * 1000, REALTIME_MAX_RECONNECT_DELAY_MS),
           },
           global: { fetch: fetchWithTimeout },
           db: { schema: 'public' },
         });
         // Replace PostgrestClient.fetch to use the cached token.
         const dataFetch = async (input: RequestInfo | URL, init: RequestInit = {}): Promise<Response> => {
           const headers = new Headers(init.headers ?? {});
           if (!headers.has('apikey')) headers.set('apikey', SUPABASE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY);
           if (!headers.has('Authorization')) {
             headers.set('Authorization', `Bearer ${cachedToken ?? SUPABASE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY}`);
           }
           return fetchWithTimeout(input as Parameters<typeof fetchWithTimeout>[0], { ...init, headers });
         };
         // eslint-disable-next-line @typescript-eslint/no-explicit-any
         (client as any).rest.fetch = dataFetch;
         client.auth.onAuthStateChange((_event, session) => {
           cached[REDACTED] ?? null;
         });
         return client;
       } catch (error: unknown) {
         handleError(error, { context: 'SupabaseClient', showToast: false });
         throw error;
       }
     }
     ```

4. Errors and fixes:
   - **navigator.locks infinite hang (FIXED)**: Every `supabase.rpc()` call went through `fetchWithAuth → _getAccessToken() → GoTrueClient.getSession() → _acquireLock(-1) → navigator.locks (exclusive)`. When a concurrent token refresh held this lock, the drag's RPC queued indefinitely. Evidence: `[fetchWithTimeout] start batch_update_timeline_frames` never appeared even though watchdog fired at 8002ms.
     - Fix: Replace `(client as any).rest.fetch` post-construction with `dataFetch` that reads `cachedToken` directly, bypassing `getSession()/navigator.locks` entirely for data requests.
   - **Confidence in diagnosis**: ~high confidence the hang was pre-`fetchWithTimeout` (definitively proven by watchdog firing without the fetch log), moderate confidence the exact trigger was a token refresh holding the lock.

5. Problem Solving:
   - **navigator.locks fix**: CONFIRMED WORKING by user
   - **Segment videos not moving with images**: Investigation in progress. Root cause identified:
     - For non-reorder moves (just shifting frame values): segments SHOULD move correctly because `pairInfo` is derived from local/optimistic positions
     - For reorder moves (image crosses past another image): structural issue — `buildDisplaySlots` matches segments to pairs by `startImage.id`. When image order changes, the pair's `startImage.id` changes. The segment's `pairShotGenerationId` references the OLD start image, which no longer starts any pair → segment disappears rather than moves
     - The `rawSegmentSlots` from `useSegmentOutputsForShot(... undefined ...)` are computed from LIVE positions, but this is secondary — the main matching uses `pairDataByIndex` (LOCAL) as the lookup key

8. Current Work:
   Investigating why segment videos don't move with images on drag. The investigation was reading through the data flow:
   - `useComputedTimelineData` correctly derives `pairInfo` and `pairDataByIndex` from LOCAL/optimistic positions
   - `useSegmentOutputStrip.buildDisplaySlots` matches segments to pairs using `pairDataByIndex.get(pair.index).startImage.id` → `byPsgId.get(startImageId)` where `byPsgId` is keyed by `slot.pairShotGenerationId`
   - The structural issue: when images are REORDERED (dragged past each other), pair 0's `startImage.id` changes. The existing segment has `pairShotGenerationId` = original start image's ID. After reorder, `byPsgId.get(newStartImageId)` finds nothing → segment disappears.
   - For NON-reorder moves (just stretching/shrinking pairs), segments should move correctly since pair indices and start images don't change — only frame values change.

   The user asked "does all this point to some kind of structural issue?" We need to complete the investigation and give a clear diagnosis. The next step is to finish analyzing whether:
   1. The issue is purely a reorder scenario (images crossing each other) OR also affects non-reorder drags
   2. Provide the user with a clear explanation of the structural issue and proposed fix
   
   Based on the current investigation, the structural issue is: segment `pairShotGenerationId` is the shot_generation_id of the START image at generation time. After reordering, `buildDisplaySlots` tries to find the segment by the NEW start image's id, which doesn't match. The fix would need to either: (a) match segments by end-image id as fallback, or (b) use the segment's stored `child_order` as a fallback index when the primary `pairShotGenerationId` lookup fails (which `useSegmentOutputsForShot` already does via the `CHILD_ORDER_STALE_FK` fallback path, but this doesn't propagate correctly to `buildDisplaySlots`).

Is it complicated to understand what's going on here? My understanding is that the position of the video should be based on the position of the chat generation image which is the same thing and that they're both in the same position coming from the same place.

> AGENT

You're right that it should be simple, and conceptually it is. Let me cut through the complexity and give you the clear picture.

## What should happen

A segment video is tethered to its start image via `pair_shot_generation_id` (a shot_generation ID). When you move the image, the video should follow. Simple.

## What actually happens

There are **two separate data streams** that both need to agree:

1. **Image positions** — come from local/optimistic state, updated immediately on drag
2. **Segment slot matching** — comes from `useSegmentOutputsForShot`, called in `useShotEditorController` with:
   ```typescript
   undefined, // localShotGenPositions not needed here
   ```
   So it always reads LIVE DB data for slot assignment.

These two streams are reconciled in `buildDisplaySlots`, which uses LOCAL pair data to find which image is at pair index 0, then looks up that image's ID in the slot map built from LIVE data. 

**For simple moves** (just stretching pair width, no crossing): both streams agree on which image is at which index → segment moves correctly ✅

**For reorders** (dragging past another image): local says "B is first", but LIVE slots are still keyed as "A is first". The lookup finds nothing and shows a placeholder ❌

## Why the comment says "not needed here"

`useShotEditorController` sits above the timeline in the component tree. The local positions live inside `Timeline.tsx`. They were never threaded up — hence the comment.

## The fix

It's not architecturally complex. `useSegmentOutputsForShot` already has full support for `localShotGenPositions` — the hook is just being called without it. We need to:

1. Expose `localShotGenPositions` from `Timeline.tsx` via an `onLocalPositionsChange` callback prop
2. Store it in `ShotEditorView` state  
3. Pass it into `useShotEditorController` → `useSegmentOutputsForShot`

All four changes are wired up correctly and types check clean. Here's the complete data flow now:

```
Timeline.tsx
  displayPositions (optimistic)
    → localShotGenPositions (rank map, computed each render)
    → imageOrderKey (stable string, only changes on reorder)
    → useEffect fires ONLY when order changes
    → calls onLocalPositionsChange(positions)

ShotImagesEditor.tsx
  handleLocalPositionsChange → setLocalShotGenPositions (state)
  localShotGenPositions → useShotImagesEditorModel(... localShotGenPositions)

useShotImagesEditorModel.ts
  useShotData(props, mode, localShotGenPositions)
    → useSegmentOutputsForShot(shotId, projectId, localShotGenPositions, ...)
      → positionMap = localShotGenPositions (instead of LIVE DB data)
      → segments get correct slot indices immediately on reorder
```

**What was broken**: after a reorder, `useSegmentOutputsForShot` was using LIVE DB positions (still showing the old order), so the `buildDisplaySlots` match failed — the segment's start-image lookup returned nothing, and a placeholder was shown instead of the video.

**What's fixed**: the moment you drag an image past another, `localShotGenPositions` is updated (rank changes), the callback fires, state updates, `useSegmentOutputsForShot` recomputes with the new order → segment correctly maps to the new pair slot. The effect is gated on `imageOrderKey` so it doesn't fire on every single frame drag tick — only when images actually cross each other.

In React 18, state updates from event handlers are batched. `setDropState` (inside async IIFE, sync part), `setPositions` (from applyOptimisticPositionUpdate), `setIsDragInProgress`, `setDragState` might all be batched.

But the async IIFE starts first: `setDropState` runs inside it. Then code AFTER the IIFE call runs: `setIsDragInProgress(false)`, `setDragState({isDragging:false})`, `currentMousePosRef.current = null`.

The batching: in React 18, all state updates in a synchronous event handler are batched (including updates inside an immediately-called async function's synchronous part). So `setDropState`, `setPositions`, `setIsDragInProgress`, `setDragState` are all batched into one render.

`currentMousePosRef.current = null` (ref mutation, not state) is also synchronous and happens in the same batch.

So in the batched render:
- `dragState.isDragging = false`
- `dropState.isDropping = true`, `frozenPositions = finalPositions`
- `positionSystem.positions = finalPositions`
- `currentMousePosRef.current = null`

- `if (!currentMousePosRef.current) return framePositions;` → returns `framePositions` = `displayPositions` = `positionSystem.positions` = finalPositions ✓

So `currentPositions` = finalPositions after drop. Segment at finalPositions.

This is consistent with no jump-back. So why does the user see a jump back?

Actually, I just noticed something in my implementation: `imageOrderKey = [...localShotGenPositions.keys()].join(',')`. But `localShotGenPositions` is a Map keyed by shotGenId and valued by position index. The keys are sorted by their insertion order (which is sorted by frame in the useMemo). So `[...localShotGenPositions.keys()]` gives the IDs in frame order (index 0 first, index 1 second, etc.).

During drag (if images cross each other), `localShotGenPositions` gets rebuilt with the new order. So `imageOrderKey` = "B's id, A's id" (if B is now before A). Callback fires, `setLocalShotGenPositions(new map)`. Then on next render, `segmentSlots` recomputes.

But there's a timing issue: `useEffect` runs AFTER paint. So the `onLocalPositionsChange` callback fires AFTER the current frame is painted. The `setLocalShotGenPositions` triggers another render. So there's at least one frame where `localShotGenPositions` is stale.

During this one frame:
- `pairInfo` = new order (LOCAL, from useComputedTimelineData with currentPositions)
- `segmentSlots` = old order (LIVE, since `localShotGenPositions` hasn't updated yet for this frame)
- `buildDisplaySlots` matches: OLD `byPsgId` (LIVE keyed) vs NEW `pairDataByIndex` (LOCAL) → mismatch → placeholder

So for ONE FRAME during a reorder, the segment appears as a placeholder (at the new pixel position). Then on the next frame it's correct.

This one-frame glitch might not be noticeable. But the "jump back when I drop" suggests something more sustained.

During 500ms freeze: `currentPositions = frozenPositions = finalPositions` ✓
After 500ms: `currentPositions = framePositions = positionSystem.positions`

If `positionSystem.positions` = finalPositions (optimistic), then no jump. ✓
If `positionSystem.positions` = OLD (if optimistic reverted or wasn't applied), then jump. ✗

Could optimistic positions have been reverted? The rollback function is called on error. If `runSerializedTimelineWrite` fails, it calls the rollback.

```typescript
await runSerializedTimelineWrite(...)
```
There's no explicit rollback call here. But in `useTimelinePositions.ts`, the rollback might be called internally.

Hmm, also looking at the timeout issue from the previous session - the RPC was taking 8+ seconds. During the 500ms freeze, the DB write would still be in progress. After 500ms, `isDropping = false`, `currentPositions = framePositions = positionSystem.positions = finalPositions` (optimistic). Segment at finalPositions. ✓

After DB write completes: `syncFromDatabase` is called. If DB positions = finalPositions, `positionSystem.positions` stays. ✓

So the jump back shouldn't happen UNLESS the optimistic update was reverted.

Actually, I want to look at this more carefully. What does `syncFromDatabase` do when positions are locked? From the log:
```
useTimelinePositions.ts:43 [TimelinePositionsPersist] syncFromDatabase suppressed (locked)
```

So during the write, `syncFromDatabase` is suppressed. After the write completes, the lock is released. Then `syncFromDatabase` might be called with fresh DB data. If the DB write succeeded, positions = finalPositions. If the write timed out (as was happening before the navigator.locks fix), positions might not be in the DB, causing a revert.

But the navigator.locks issue is now fixed. So RPC should complete quickly and `syncFromDatabase` would show finalPositions. ✓

The "jump back" they described might be the manifestation of the slot mismatch: segment disappears (placeholder) at one position, then re-appears at wrong position when LIVE data updates.

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **Two data streams**: (1) Local/optimistic positions from `usePositionManagement` → `displayPositions` for image nodes; (2) LIVE DB positions from `useSegmentOutputsForShot` → `segmentSlots` for segment strip
   - **`buildDisplaySlots`**: Matches segments to pairs using `pairDataByIndex.get(pair.index).startImage.id` (LOCAL) keyed against `byPsgId` (keyed by `slot.pairShotGenerationId` = LIVE start image IDs)
   - **Reorder mismatch**: After a reorder, LOCAL `pairDataByIndex[0].startImage.id = B` but LIVE `byPsgId` is keyed by `A's id` → no match → segment disappears or appears at wrong pair
   - **`localShotGenPositions`**: Map of `shot_generation_id → 0-based position index` computed from LOCAL frame positions, used to drive `useSegmentOutputsForShot` slot assignment
   - **`imageOrderKey`**: Stable string of ordered shot_gen IDs — only changes when images actually cross (reorder), NOT on every frame-drag tick — prevents flooding parent with state updates
   - **`pairShotGenerationId` in slots**: Set to `liveStartImage?.id` (LIVE DB start image at slot index), which is the key used in `byPsgId` lookup
   - **Drag preview vs frozen vs frame positions**: `calculateDragPreview()` returns `framePositions` when `!currentMousePosRef.current`, else checks `dropState.isDropping` for `frozenPositions`, else live drag preview
   - **navigator.locks bypass** (from prior session, confirmed fixed): `PostgrestClient.fetch` replaced post-construction to use cached token directly, bypassing `GoTrueClient.getSession() → _acquireLock(-1)`

     - Added `imageOrderKey` (stable string dep — only changes on actual reorder, not frame shifts)

     ```typescript
         .filter(([id]) => id !== TRAILING_ENDPOINT_KEY)
         .sort((a, b) => a[1] - b[1])
         .forEach(([id], index) => map.set(id, index));
       return map;

       () => [...localShotGenPositions.keys()].join(','),
       [localShotGenPositions],
     );
     
     const localShotGenPositionsRef = useRef(localShotGenPositions);
     localShotGenPositionsRef.current = localShotGenPositions;
     
     useEffect(() => {
       onLocalPositionsChange?.(localShotGenPositionsRef.current);
     // imageOrderKey triggers only on actual reorders; onLocalPositionsChange is stable (useCallback).
     // eslint-disable-next-line react-hooks/exhaustive-deps
     }, [imageOrderKey, onLocalPositionsChange]);
     ```

## Answering your questions directly

**Why is this so complicated?**

The complexity comes from two independent position systems that need to stay in sync:
1. **Image nodes** → driven by `displayPositions` (optimistic, follows your mouse in real time)
2. **Segment strips** → driven by `segmentSlots`, which was using **live DB positions** to figure out *which slot each segment belongs to*

These diverge the moment you reorder locally and haven't written to the DB yet. My fix bridges them by passing the local order through to the slot matcher.

**The jump-back you're seeing** is exactly that divergence. During drag: local positions drive both → segments follow. On drop: DB invalidation fires, segment slot matcher reverts to live order briefly → segment jumps back even though the image (driven purely by local positions) stays put.

**Good logs to filter**

In the browser console, filter for `[SegmentOutputsForShot]`. Look for:

- `slot build start` — confirms `useLocalPositions: true` (means my fix is active) vs `false` (old behavior)
- `localOrderMismatch` / `localCountMismatch` — tells you if local and live still disagree at build time
- `slot build result` → `mapping` — shows which segment ended up at which slot index (or `placeholder`)
- `liveTimeline fetched` — fires when DB positions refresh (this is what triggers the jump)

**To verify the fix is working**, drag an image past another and watch for:
```
[SegmentOutputsForShot] slot build start { useLocalPositions: true, ... }
```

If you see `useLocalPositions: false` after my changes are in effect, the `localShotGenPositions` state isn't reaching the hook — let me know and I'll trace why.

Try it out and share what you see in the logs (or if the jump is gone).

Good. I have the complete picture now. This is a **single bug in one place**, not needless complexity.

**Root cause**: In the fill loop (line 602 of `useSegmentOutputsForShot.ts`), `pairShotGenerationId` is always set from `liveStartImage?.id` (LIVE timeline data). But `buildDisplaySlots` in `useSegmentOutputStrip.ts` matches slots to LOCAL pairs by `LOCAL_pair_i.startImage.id`. After a reorder, these don't agree → segments misplace.

The fix: for child slots, use `child.pair_shot_generation_id` directly (that IS the correct start image ID), not the LIVE image at that slot index.

That's the fix — four lines changed in one file. To directly answer your question: single bug, not needless complexity. The two-position-system architecture (local optimistic vs live DB) is the right design for snappy drag UX. The bug was simply that the slot-matching key (`pairShotGenerationId`) was computed from LIVE timeline order instead of the child's own identity.

**What this fixes**: After a drag reorder, each child segment now carries its correct `pair_shot_generation_id` (the start image it belongs to). `buildDisplaySlots` looks up by the LOCAL pair's start image ID, which now matches, so the video follows the image to its new position.
